People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

schedules, invoices, and apprentice questions all pile up on my bench until I’m rushing and still missing deadlines.\n- key: S007\n  name: Losing_Items\n  description: Often loses things necessary for tasks or activities.\n  manifestation: I’m constantly hunting for my keys, tape measure, phone, order sheets, or the notes I just had in my hand, and it eats up so much of the day that the guys have manifestation: What brings me in is I cannot keep my mind on one thing anymore—one saw starting up, somebody walking past the office, or even a thought about another job will pull me off what I’m People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. It’s… frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with focus and restlessness, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Even in grade school, before I was 12, teachers would tell my mom I was bright but careless. I’d lose homework, forget instructions… stare out the window instead of finishing my work.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often have trouble playing quietly or engaging in leisure activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I wasn’t very good at being still for quiet things. Even now, honestly, if it’s church or just sitting at home, I’ll start tapping or shifting around… sometimes whisper to my husband or get up when I don’t really need to. The quiet makes it hard to just sit there.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often run, climb, or play more than other kids?\n\nPatient: I don’t remember the kid part real clearly. I mean… these days, if I have to sit at a desk doing estimates or sit through a long meeting, I get real wound up inside, like I need to get up and move. Even when I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your difficulties with focus and restlessness have significantly impacted your overall well-being or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I don’t like admitting it, but at work my mistakes and being late on things have cost me commissions. Sometimes jobs have to be redone, and the crew doesn’t really count on me as much for scheduling or ordering anymore.\n\nAt home… my husband handles the bills and reminders now. I’ll misplace papers or miss what needs doing. It’s caused problems, yes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I mean… these days, if I have to sit at a desk doing estimates or sit through a long meeting, I get real wound up inside, like I need to get up and move. Even when I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your difficulties with focus and restlessness have significantly impacted your overall well-being or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I don’t like admitting it, but at work my mistakes and being late on things have cost me commissions. Sometimes jobs have to be redone, and the crew doesn’t really count on me as much for scheduling or ordering anymore.\n\nAt home… my husband handles the bills and reminders now. I’ll misplace papers or miss what needs doing. It’s caused problems, yes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing when you're doing things you enjoy or find interesting?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even if I’m interested, I still get pulled off. Like… one saw starts up, or somebody walks past the office, or I just think of another job, and I lose the thread. Then I look up and the day’s gone sideways.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly on the go, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. It’s more when I’m stuck sitting, like at the desk doing estimates or in a long meeting. I get wound up inside, like I need to get up and move around, even if I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you didn't have these focus and restlessness issues?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I’ll misplace papers or miss what needs doing. It’s caused problems, yes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing when you're doing things you enjoy or find interesting?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even if I’m interested, I still get pulled off. Like… one saw starts up, or somebody walks past the office, or I just think of another job, and I lose the thread. Then I look up and the day’s gone sideways.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly on the go, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. It’s more when I’m stuck sitting, like at the desk doing estimates or in a long meeting. I get wound up inside, like I need to get up and move around, even if I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you didn't have these focus and restlessness issues?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… as far back as grade school, teachers were telling my mother I was bright but careless. Losing homework, forgetting what they’d told me to do, staring out the window instead of finishing my work. That was before I was 12, so… it’s kind of always been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with focus or restlessness in multiple areas of your life, such as at work, at home, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s not just work.\n\nOn jobs, if I miss something or drag my feet too long, it can cost me commissions, or we have to redo things. The crew… they don’t really count on me much for scheduling or ordering anymore.\n\nAnd at home, my husband handles most of the bills and reminders now. I lose papers or forget what needs doing. I don’t like saying that, but… yeah, it’s in more than one place.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. (Note: In adolescents or adults, may be limited to feeling restless.)\n  manifestation: If I have to sit at the desk doing estimates or sit through a long meeting, I feel wound up inside like I need to get up and move around, even though my wrist is healed and I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n- key: S013\n  name: Quiet_Activity_Difficulty\n  description: Often unable to play or engage in leisure activities quietly.\n  manifestation: Even at home or in church, when things are supposed to be quiet, I catch myself tapping, shifting around, whispering to my husband, or getting up for no real reason because sitting still in the quiet feels almost unbearable.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: Onset prior to age 12 years\n  manifestation: Even back in grade school, before I was 12, teachers were always telling my mother I was bright but careless, losing homework, forgetting instructions, and staring out the window instead of finishing my seatwork.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: My mistakes and delays have cost me commissions, caused rework on jobs, and made the crew less willing to rely on me for scheduling or ordering. At home, my husband has had to take over bills and reminders because I keep misplacing papers or missing what needs to be done.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 months\n  manifestation: This level of trouble has been going on for about a year and a month, since I broke my wrist and got shifted into shop paperwork and supervising instead of hands-on carpentry.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 57 / carpenter / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S008\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you didn't have these focus and restlessness issues?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… as far back as grade school, teachers were telling my mother I was bright but careless. Losing homework, forgetting what they’d told me to do, staring out the window instead of finishing my work. That was before I was 12, so… it’s kind of always been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with focus or restlessness in multiple areas of your life, such as at work, at home, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s not just work.\n\nOn jobs, if I miss something or drag my feet too long, it can cost me commissions, or we have to redo things. The crew… they don’t really count on me much for scheduling or ordering anymore.\n\nAnd at home, my husband handles most of the bills and reminders now. I lose papers or forget what needs doing. I don’t like saying that, but… yeah, it’s in more than one place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and restlessness have caused problems in your relationships with friends?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I don’t have a huge circle of friends. At work, one saw starts up, or someone walks by, or I think of another job I need to do, and I’m off track. Then the whole day just kind of gets away from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Mostly if I’m stuck sitting, like doing estimates at the desk or in a long meeting. I get kind of wound up inside, like I need to get up and move, even when I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really, I guess. I mean, I might do things too quick sometimes, but I don’t think of myself as someone who’s always acting without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing at home or in your free time, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s not only work. At home too, I’ll be trying to do one thing and then some noise, or someone walking by, or just another thought pops in, and I’m off. I don’t like admitting it, but at work my mistakes and being late on things have cost me commissions. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m just… having a hard time keeping my mind on one thing. At work, one saw starts up, or someone walks by, or I think of another job I need to do, and I’m off track. Then the whole day just kind of gets away from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Mostly if I’m stuck sitting, like doing estimates at the desk or in a long meeting. I get kind of wound up inside, like I need to get up and move, even when I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really, I guess. I mean, I might do things too quick sometimes, but I don’t think of myself as someone who’s always acting without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing at home or in your free time, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s not only work. At home too, I’ll be trying to do one thing and then some noise, or someone walking by, or just another thought pops in, and I’m off. Then I look up and I haven’t really finished anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your difficulties with focus and restlessness affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I don’t like talking about it much, but at work, when I miss things or get behind, it’s caused rework and lost me commissions. The crew doesn’t really count on me as much for scheduling or ordering now.\n\nAt home, my husband’s had to take over bills and reminders because I’ll misplace papers or miss what needs doing. It’s… frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with focus and restlessness, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Even in grade school, before I was 12, teachers would tell my mom I was bright but careless. I’d lose homework, forget instructions… stare out the window instead of finishing my work.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often have trouble playing quietly or engaging in leisure activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I wasn’t very good at being still for quiet things. Even now, honestly, if it’s church or just sitting at home, I’ll start tapping or shifting around… sometimes whisper to my husband or get up when I don’t really need to. The quiet makes it hard to just sit there.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often run, climb, or play more than other kids?\n\nPatient: I don’t remember the kid part real clearly. I mean… these days, if I have to sit at a desk doing estimates or sit through a long meeting, I get real wound up inside, like I need to get up and move. Even when I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your difficulties with focus and restlessness have significantly impacted your overall well-being or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I don’t like admitting it, but at work my mistakes and being late on things have cost me commissions. Sometimes jobs have to be redone, and the crew doesn’t really count on me as much for scheduling or ordering anymore.\n\nAt home… my husband handles the bills and reminders now. I’ll misplace papers or miss what needs doing. It’s caused problems, yes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing when you're doing things you enjoy or find interesting?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even if I’m interested, I still get pulled off. Like… one saw starts up, or somebody walks past the office, or I just think of another job, and I lose the thread. Then I look up and the day’s gone sideways.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly on the go, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. It’s more when I’m stuck sitting, like at the desk doing estimates or in a long meeting. I get wound up inside, like I need to get up and move around, even if I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you didn't have these focus and restlessness issues?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… as far back as grade school, teachers were telling my mother I was bright but careless. Losing homework, forgetting what they’d told me to do, staring out the window instead of finishing my work. That was before I was 12, so… it’s kind of always been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with focus or restlessness in multiple areas of your life, such as at work, at home, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s not just work.\n\nOn jobs, if I miss something or drag my feet too long, it can cost me commissions, or we have to redo things. The crew… they don’t really count on me much for scheduling or ordering anymore.\n\nAnd at home, my husband handles most of the bills and reminders now. I lose papers or forget what needs doing. I don’t like saying that, but… yeah, it’s in more than one place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and restlessness have caused problems in your relationships with friends?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I don’t have a huge circle of friends. I suppose maybe I’ve missed things or not kept up the way I should, but I can’t say for sure it’s caused big problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with focus or paying attention in both work and social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly at work, but it happens around people too.\n\nWhat brings me in is I just can’t keep my mind on one thing anymore. A saw starts up, somebody walks past the office, or I’ll think of another job for a second, and then I’m off track. By the time I notice, half the day’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, maybe once in a while like anyone, but I don’t think that’s a big thing for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I get kind of wound up inside, like I need to get up and move, even when I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really, I guess. I mean, I might do things too quick sometimes, but I don’t think of myself as someone who’s always acting without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing at home or in your free time, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s not only work. At home too, I’ll be trying to do one thing and then some noise, or someone walking by, or just another thought pops in, and I’m off. Then I look up and I haven’t really finished anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your difficulties with focus and restlessness affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I don’t like talking about it much, but at work, when I miss things or get behind, it’s caused rework and lost me commissions. The crew doesn’t really count on me as much for scheduling or ordering now.\n\nAt home, my husband’s had to take over bills and reminders because I’ll misplace papers or miss what needs doing. It’s… frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with focus and restlessness, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Even in grade school, before I was 12, teachers would tell my mom I was bright but careless. I’d lose homework, forget instructions… stare out the window instead of finishing my work.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often have trouble playing quietly or engaging in leisure activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I wasn’t very good at being still for quiet things. Even now, honestly, if it’s church or just sitting at home, I’ll start tapping or shifting around… sometimes whisper to my husband or get up when I don’t really need to. The quiet makes it hard to just sit there.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often run, climb, or play more than other kids?\n\nPatient: I don’t remember the kid part real clearly. I mean… these days, if I have to sit at a desk doing estimates or sit through a long meeting, I get real wound up inside, like I need to get up and move. Even when I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your difficulties with focus and restlessness have significantly impacted your overall well-being or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I don’t like admitting it, but at work my mistakes and being late on things have cost me commissions. Sometimes jobs have to be redone, and the crew doesn’t really count on me as much for scheduling or ordering anymore.\n\nAt home… my husband handles the bills and reminders now. I’ll misplace papers or miss what needs doing. It’s caused problems, yes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing when you're doing things you enjoy or find interesting?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even if I’m interested, I still get pulled off. Like… one saw starts up, or somebody walks past the office, or I just think of another job, and I lose the thread. Then I look up and the day’s gone sideways.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly on the go, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. It’s more when I’m stuck sitting, like at the desk doing estimates or in a long meeting. I get wound up inside, like I need to get up and move around, even if I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you didn't have these focus and restlessness issues?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… as far back as grade school, teachers were telling my mother I was bright but careless. Losing homework, forgetting what they’d told me to do, staring out the window instead of finishing my work. That was before I was 12, so… it’s kind of always been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with focus or restlessness in multiple areas of your life, such as at work, at home, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s not just work.\n\nOn jobs, if I miss something or drag my feet too long, it can cost me commissions, or we have to redo things. The crew… they don’t really count on me much for scheduling or ordering anymore.\n\nAnd at home, my husband handles most of the bills and reminders now. I lose papers or forget what needs doing. I don’t like saying that, but… yeah, it’s in more than one place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and restlessness have caused problems in your relationships with friends?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I don’t have a huge circle of friends. I suppose maybe I’ve missed things or not kept up the way I should, but I can’t say for sure it’s caused big problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with focus or paying attention in both work and social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly at work, but it happens around people too.\n\nWhat brings me in is I just can’t keep my mind on one thing anymore. A saw starts up, somebody walks past the office, or I’ll think of another job for a second, and then I’m off track. By the time I notice, half the day’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, maybe once in a while like anyone, but I don’t think that’s a big thing for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often blur out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I might jump in now and then, but I don’t think I’m usually blurting out answers before someone finishes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

It’s… frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with focus and restlessness, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Even in grade school, before I was 12, teachers would tell my mom I was bright but careless. I’d lose homework, forget instructions… stare out the window instead of finishing my work.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often have trouble playing quietly or engaging in leisure activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I wasn’t very good at being still for quiet things. Even now, honestly, if it’s church or just sitting at home, I’ll start tapping or shifting around… sometimes whisper to my husband or get up when I don’t really need to. The quiet makes it hard to just sit there.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often run, climb, or play more than other kids?\n\nPatient: I don’t remember the kid part real clearly. I mean… these days, if I have to sit at a desk doing estimates or sit through a long meeting, I get real wound up inside, like I need to get up and move. Even when I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your difficulties with focus and restlessness have significantly impacted your overall well-being or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I don’t like admitting it, but at work my mistakes and being late on things have cost me commissions. Sometimes jobs have to be redone, and the crew doesn’t really count on me as much for scheduling or ordering anymore.\n\nAt home… my husband handles the bills and reminders now. I’ll misplace papers or miss what needs doing. It’s caused problems, yes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing when you're doing things you enjoy or find interesting?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even if I’m interested, I still get pulled off. Like… one saw starts up, or somebody walks past the office, or I just think of another job, and I lose the thread. Then I look up and the day’s gone sideways.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly on the go, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. It’s more when I’m stuck sitting, like at the desk doing estimates or in a long meeting. I get wound up inside, like I need to get up and move around, even if I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you didn't have these focus and restlessness issues?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… as far back as grade school, teachers were telling my mother I was bright but careless. Losing homework, forgetting what they’d told me to do, staring out the window instead of finishing my work. That was before I was 12, so… it’s kind of always been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with focus or restlessness in multiple areas of your life, such as at work, at home, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s not just work.\n\nOn jobs, if I miss something or drag my feet too long, it can cost me commissions, or we have to redo things. The crew… they don’t really count on me much for scheduling or ordering anymore.\n\nAnd at home, my husband handles most of the bills and reminders now. I lose papers or forget what needs doing. I don’t like saying that, but… yeah, it’s in more than one place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and restlessness have caused problems in your relationships with friends?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I don’t have a huge circle of friends. I suppose maybe I’ve missed things or not kept up the way I should, but I can’t say for sure it’s caused big problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with focus or paying attention in both work and social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly at work, but it happens around people too.\n\nWhat brings me in is I just can’t keep my mind on one thing anymore. A saw starts up, somebody walks past the office, or I’ll think of another job for a second, and then I’m off track. By the time I notice, half the day’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, maybe once in a while like anyone, but I don’t think that’s a big thing for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often blur out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I might jump in now and then, but I don’t think I’m usually blurting out answers before someone finishes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I try not to. I’m not saying I never make a quick decision, but I wouldn’t call that a regular problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty with organization and time management?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one’s been pretty bad. Since I’ve been stuck doing more of the shop paperwork, I can’t seem to sort what needs doing first. The bids, schedules, invoices, apprentices asking questions… it all just piles up on my bench, and then I’m rushing and still missing deadlines.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

Then I look up and the day’s gone sideways.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly on the go, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. It’s more when I’m stuck sitting, like at the desk doing estimates or in a long meeting. I get wound up inside, like I need to get up and move around, even if I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you didn't have these focus and restlessness issues?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… as far back as grade school, teachers were telling my mother I was bright but careless. Losing homework, forgetting what they’d told me to do, staring out the window instead of finishing my work. That was before I was 12, so… it’s kind of always been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with focus or restlessness in multiple areas of your life, such as at work, at home, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s not just work.\n\nOn jobs, if I miss something or drag my feet too long, it can cost me commissions, or we have to redo things. The crew… they don’t really count on me much for scheduling or ordering anymore.\n\nAnd at home, my husband handles most of the bills and reminders now. I lose papers or forget what needs doing. I don’t like saying that, but… yeah, it’s in more than one place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and restlessness have caused problems in your relationships with friends?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I don’t have a huge circle of friends. I suppose maybe I’ve missed things or not kept up the way I should, but I can’t say for sure it’s caused big problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with focus or paying attention in both work and social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly at work, but it happens around people too.\n\nWhat brings me in is I just can’t keep my mind on one thing anymore. A saw starts up, somebody walks past the office, or I’ll think of another job for a second, and then I’m off track. By the time I notice, half the day’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, maybe once in a while like anyone, but I don’t think that’s a big thing for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often blur out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I might jump in now and then, but I don’t think I’m usually blurting out answers before someone finishes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I try not to. I’m not saying I never make a quick decision, but I wouldn’t call that a regular problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty with organization and time management?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one’s been pretty bad. Since I’ve been stuck doing more of the shop paperwork, I can’t seem to sort what needs doing first. The bids, schedules, invoices, apprentices asking questions… it all just piles up on my bench, and then I’m rushing and still missing deadlines.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been diagnosed with attention deficit hyperactivity disorder or does anyone in your family have it?\n\nPatient: No, not that I know of. I’ve never been told I had it, and… I don’t really know about anyone in my family."
